**mise.toml** (`/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/mise.toml`):

  - `test` - Run all tests with `go test ./...`
  - `test:git-http-backend` - Optional integration tests with git-http-backend
  - `test:linux-smoke` - Optional live Linux bootstrap smoke test (60m timeout)
  - `test:linux-smoke:batched` - Optional batched Linux bootstrap test

### 4. Project Structure

**Root binaries:**
- `/cmd/git-sync/` - Main CLI application
- `/cmd/git-sync-bench/` - Benchmarking tool

- `/internal/auth/` - Authentication & credential handling
- `/internal/convert/` - Conversion utilities
- `/internal/gitproto/` - Git protocol implementation (smart HTTP, fetch, push, refs, pktline)
- `/internal/planner/` - Planning/orchestration logic
- `/internal/strategy/` - Multiple strategies:
  - `bootstrap/` - Initial bootstrap strategy
  - `incremental/` - Incremental sync strategy
  - `materialized/` - Materialized checkout strategy
  - `replicate/` - Full replication strategy
- `/internal/syncer/` - Core sync orchestration
- `/internal/syncertest/` - Test utilities
- `/internal/validation/` - Validation logic

**Public API:**
- `/pkg/gitsync/` - Public package exported as git-sync library

Tests are distributed throughout the codebase as `*_test.go` files. Key test files:

Test execution via:

## GitHub Actions CI Setup - Entire CLI Repository

### Summary
The Entire CLI is a **Go-based CLI application** (Go 1.26.1) with comprehensive CI/CD using GitHub Actions. The project uses **mise** as a task runner and **GoReleaser Pro** for releases. The CI/CD pipeline has robust testing, linting, and E2E testing across multiple agent platforms.

---

## 1. All Workflow Files

The repository has 7 workflow files in `.github/workflows/`:

1. **ci.yml** - Unit & integration tests with race detection
2. **lint.yml** - Go linting and code quality checks
3. **license-check.yml** - Reusable workflow for license compliance
4. **e2e.yml** - End-to-end tests across multiple AI agents
5. **e2e-isolated.yml** - Single agent E2E test (manual/debugging)
6. **e2e-windows.yml** - Windows-specific E2E tests
7. **release.yml** - Release automation with binary signing

---

## 2. Workflow Details

### **ci.yml** - Tests
**Triggers:** `pull_request`, `push` to `main`, `workflow_dispatch`  
**Runner:** `ubuntu-latest`  
**Jobs:**
- `test` job:
  - Checks out PR head SHA (important for PR validation)
  - Sets up gnome-keyring (credential storage for tests)
  - Uses `jdx/mise-action@v4` to install tools
  - Runs: `mise run test:ci` — executes unit tests, integration tests, and E2E canary with race detection

**Dependencies:** Requires gnome-keyring system package

**File:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.github/workflows/ci.yml`

---

### **lint.yml** - Code Quality
**Triggers:** `pull_request`, `push` to `main`, `workflow_dispatch`  
**Runner:** `ubuntu-latest`  
**Permissions:** `contents: read`  
**Jobs:**
- `lint` job (2 steps):
  - Checks out PR head SHA
  - Sets up Go stable version
  - Uses `jdx/mise-action@v4`
  - Runs: `mise run lint` — composite lint task (see section 3)
  - **Inline feedback step:** Runs `golangci/golangci-lint-action@v9` (v2.11.3) for GitHub UI integration
    - Same config as `mise run lint:go` but provides inline PR annotations

**File:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.github/workflows/lint.yml`

---

### **license-check.yml** - License Compliance
**Triggers:** `pull_request`, `push` to `main`, `workflow_dispatch`  
**Reusable:** Uses shared Entire workflow: `entireio/shared/.github/workflows/license-check-reusable.yml@main`

**File:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.github/workflows/license-check.yml`

---

### **e2e.yml** - E2E Tests (Multi-Agent)
**Triggers:** `push` to `main`, `workflow_dispatch` with agent selector  
**Concurrency:** Only one E2E job runs at a time (cancels previous runs)  
**Permissions:** `actions: read`, `contents: read`  

**Workflow Structure:**
- **matrix-setup** job: Dynamically builds agent list based on input
  - Manual dispatch can run single agent (claude-code, opencode, gemini-cli, factoryai-droid, cursor-cli, copilot-cli, roger-roger, codex)
  - Default runs all 8 agents

- **e2e-tests** job (matrix across agents):
  - Timeout: 40 minutes
  - `fail-fast: false` (all agents test even if one fails)
  - Steps:
    1. Checkout & setup mise
    2. Install tmux (required for agent testing)
    3. Install agent CLI dynamically (curl/npm based on agent type)
    4. Bootstrap agent with API keys (skipped for roger-roger)
    5. Run E2E tests: `mise run test:e2e --agent <agent>`
    6. Upload test artifacts (7-day retention)
  - Environment variables passed: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, GEMINI_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, CURSOR_API_KEY, FACTORY_API_KEY, COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN
  - E2E_CONCURRENT_TEST_LIMIT set per agent (gemini-cli=6, factoryai-droid=1)

- **e2e-windows** job:
  - Calls workflow_call to `e2e-windows.yml` (reuses Windows workflow)
  - Inherits secrets

- **notify-slack** job:
  - Runs only if E2E fails on push to main
  - Sends Slack notification with failed agents list
  - Uses slackapi/slack-github-action@v3.0.1

**File:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.github/workflows/e2e.yml`

---

### **e2e-isolated.yml** - Single Agent E2E (Debug/Manual)
**Triggers:** `workflow_dispatch` (manual only)  
**Inputs:** Agent (required, default=gemini-cli), Test filter (regex, default=TestInteractiveMultiStep)  
**Runner:** `ubuntu-latest`  
**Timeout:** 20 minutes  

**Steps:**
1. Checkout
2. Setup mise
3. Install tmux
4. Build entire CLI from source: `go build -o /usr/local/bin/entire ./cmd/entire`
5. Install agent CLI (same logic as e2e.yml)
6. Bootstrap agent (skipped for roger-roger)
7. Run isolated test: `mise run test:e2e --agent <agent> "<test_filter>"`
8. Upload artifacts

**Use case:** Debug single test with specific agent without running full suite

**File:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.github/workflows/e2e-isolated.yml`

---

### **e2e-windows.yml** - Windows E2E Tests
**Triggers:** `workflow_call` (from e2e.yml), `workflow_dispatch`, scheduled nightly at 6am UTC  
**Runner:** `windows-latest`  
**Timeout:** 45 minutes  
**Concurrency:** Only one Windows test at a time  

**Steps:**
1. Checkout
2. Setup mise
3. Install Claude Code via PowerShell: `irm https://claude.ai/install.ps1 | iex`
4. Add to PATH
5. Bootstrap agent: `go run ./e2e/bootstrap claude-code`
6. Run tests (shell: bash):
   - E2E_PARALLEL=1 (sequential on Windows)
   - `mise run test:e2e --agent claude-code`
7. Upload artifacts

**Special:** Windows-only, claude-code agent only, sequential testing (E2E_PARALLEL=1)

**File:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.github/workflows/e2e-windows.yml`

---

### **release.yml** - Release Automation
**Triggers:** `push` with tags matching `v*`, `workflow_dispatch`  
**Runner:** `ubuntu-latest`  
**Permissions:** `contents: write`  

**Jobs:**
- **release**:
  - Checkout with full history
  - Setup mise
  - Generate Homebrew App token (for tap/scoop updates)
  - Extract release notes from CHANGELOG.md (fails if no entry for version)
  - Run GoReleaser Pro:
    - Builds binaries for darwin/linux/windows, amd64/arm64
    - MacOS code signing & notarization (if secrets configured)
    - Archives (tar.gz for Unix, zip for Windows)
    - Publishes to Homebrew tap + Scoop bucket
    - Announces to Discord
  - Environment: GITHUB_TOKEN, TAP_GITHUB_TOKEN, GORELEASER_KEY, POSTHOG_*, MACOS_SIGN_*, DISCORD_*

- **notify-slack**:
  - Runs if release fails
  - Sends Slack notification with failure details

**File:** `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/.github/workflows/release.yml`

## 3. PR Gates (Checks That Must Pass Before Merging)

Based on workflow triggers and typical GitHub branch protection, these are the PR checks:

1. **Tests (ci.yml)** - `test` job
   - Unit tests with race detection
   - Integration tests
   - E2E canary tests
   - Status: Required (runs on all PRs)

2. **Lint (lint.yml)** - `lint` job
   - golangci-lint (2.11.3) with inline feedback
   - Go code formatting (gofmt)
   - Shell script linting (shellcheck)
   - Go module tidiness (go mod tidy)
   - Mise task file checks
   - Status: Required (runs on all PRs)

3. **License Check** - `check-licenses` job
   - Reusable workflow from shared repo
   - Status: Likely required (common practice at Entire)

**Note:** E2E tests (e2e.yml, e2e-windows.yml) run on `push` to `main` only, not PRs (no `pull_request` trigger), so they are NOT PR gates but post-merge quality checks.

## 5. Language & Tooling Specifics

**Language:** Go 1.26.1 (managed via mise)

**Build System:** mise (task runner)

**Package Manager:** Go modules (go.mod/go.sum)

**Go-Specific CI Steps:**

1. **Linting** (`.golangci.yaml` — very strict)
   - 60+ linters enabled (asciicheck, bodyclose, gosec, govet, revive, etc.)
   - Custom forbid rules (prevents os.Getwd, unsafe go-git operations)
   - Duplication threshold: 75 tokens (strict)
   - Requires specific linter explanations (nolintlint)
   - E2E tests excluded from some checks
   - **Run locally & in CI:** `mise run lint` (depends on: lint:go, lint:gofmt, lint:gomod, lint:mise, lint:shellcheck)

2. **Testing**
   - Unit tests: `go test ./...`
   - Integration tests: `go test -tags=integration ./cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/...`
   - Race detection: `-race` flag on CI
   - E2E: Custom test framework with fixtures (agents, sessions, etc.)
   - Test runner: gotestsum (for formatted output)
   - E2E artifacts: JSON test events + HTML reports
   - **Canary tests** (deterministic, no API calls): vogon agent (built-in) + roger-roger (external)

3. **Building**
   - Cross-platform: darwin (amd64, arm64), linux (amd64, arm64), windows (amd64, arm64)
   - Development build: Version injected via ldflags
   - Mise task: `mise run build`
   - GoReleaser: Pro edition for macOS signing/notarization

4. **Release Process** (Go-specific)
   - GoReleaser Pro handles binary building, signing, notarization
   - Archives: tar.gz (Unix), zip (Windows)
   - Publishes to Homebrew tap & Scoop bucket
   - Discord announcement
   - Requires CHANGELOG.md entry matching git tag

5. **Dependency Management**
   - Dependabot monitors go.mod (daily updates)
   - Groups: Minor/patch together, major updates separate
   - Max 10 go-mod PRs, 5 github-actions PRs at once

**Non-Go-Specific/Generic CI:**

- **System setup:** gnome-keyring (credential store), tmux (terminal multiplexer)
- **Agent testing:** Works with 8 different AI CLI agents (language-agnostic)
- **Artifact storage:** 7-day retention (standard GitHub practice)
- **Secrets management:** API keys for multiple platforms (Anthropic, Google, OpenAI, etc.)
- **Notifications:** Slack on E2E/release failures (generic pattern)
- **Code owners:** Standard GitHub feature

Here's the overview:

## CLI repo PR gates (what runs on every PR)

| Workflow | What it does |
|----------|-------------|
| **ci.yml** | Unit + integration tests with `-race` detection, via `mise run test:ci` |
| **lint.yml** | golangci-lint (60+ linters, inline PR annotations), gofmt check, `go mod tidy` check, shellcheck, mise task validation |
| **license-check.yml** | Reusable workflow from `entireio/shared` for license compliance |

E2E tests only run on push to `main`, not as PR gates (too expensive/flaky).

## git-sync current state

**No CI at all** - no `.github/workflows/` directory exists. But the building blocks are ready in `mise.toml`:
- `mise run test` → `go test ./...`
- `mise run fmt` → `gofmt -s -w`
- `golangci-lint` 2.11.3 installed (but no `.golangci.yml` config file)
- `shellcheck` installed

## What I'd propose for git-sync PR gates

| Workflow | Based on CLI's | Notes |
|----------|----------------|-------|
| **ci.yml** — Tests | Same pattern | `go test -race ./...` — skip the smoke/integration tests that need external services (`test:git-http-backend`, `test:entire-local-smoke`, etc.) |
| **lint.yml** — Linting | Same pattern | golangci-lint + gofmt check + `go mod tidy` check. We'd need to create a `.golangci.yml` — we could start with a reasonable subset of the CLI's 60+ linters, or copy it wholesale. Also add the `golangci-lint-action` for inline PR annotations. |
| **license-check.yml** | Direct reuse | Same shared workflow from `entireio/shared` |

**Things to decide:**

1. **Linter strictness** — Start with a copy of the CLI's `.golangci.yml` (may surface existing violations to fix) or start lighter and tighten over time?
2. **Race detection** — The CLI runs tests with `-race` in CI. Worth doing here too? (Slight slowdown but catches real bugs in concurrent code.)
3. **gnome-keyring** — git-sync uses `go-keyring` like the CLI. The CLI's CI sets up gnome-keyring on Ubuntu for this. We'd likely need the same if any tests touch auth/keyring code.
4. **License check** — Want to include the shared `entireio/shared` license check workflow?

What's your call on these?

```
Now let me create a final comprehensive report:

## Summary of Test Code and Keyring Usage

Based on my thorough exploration of `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync`, here are my findings:

### 1. Keyring References in Test Files

**Files that import/use `go-keyring`:**
- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/auth/auth_test.go` (line 17)
- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/entire_local_smoke_test.go` (line 15)

**Usage Details:**

In `internal/auth/auth_test.go`:
- The file imports `github.com/zalando/go-keyring` 
- Uses `keyring.ErrNotFound` error type in multiple tests (lines 498, 523, 529, 543-544)
- Tests validate that keyring errors are properly detected and handled
- The tests are **unit tests that don't require the actual keyring system** because they mock file-based token storage with `ENTIRE_TOKEN_STORE=file` environment variable

In `internal/syncer/entire_local_smoke_test.go`:
- Imports `keyring` (line 15)
- Uses `keyring.Get()` to retrieve tokens from keyring in function `lookupEntireToken()` (line 255)
- **This is an integration/e2e test that requires the actual system keyring** - it's gated behind `GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIRE=1` environment variable (line 34)
- Only runs when explicitly enabled for Entire local integration testing

### 2. Auth Package Structure

**Files in `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/auth/`:**

1. **auth.go** (120 lines)
   - Core authentication logic
   - `Resolve()` function: resolves auth method in order: explicit flags → Entire DB token → git credential helper → anonymous
   - `explicitAuth()`: handles bearer tokens and basic auth tokens
   - `lookupGitCredential()`: calls git credential helper (mocked for tests)
   - Exports `GitCredentialFillCommand` for test mocking

2. **tokenstore.go** (143 lines)
   - Token storage abstraction with two backends:
     - **Keyring backend** (default): Uses `keyring.Get()` and `keyring.Set()`
     - **File backend**: Uses JSON file at `~/.config/entiredb/tokens.json` with file locking
   - Functions: `ReadStoredToken()`, `WriteStoredToken()`, `readFileToken()`, `writeFileToken()`
   - Environment variables control backend: `ENTIRE_TOKEN_STORE=file` switches to file backend
   - File locking using `syscall.Flock()` for concurrent safety

3. **entiredb.go** (224 lines)
   - Handles Entire DB credential lookup and token refresh
   - `LookupEntireDBCredential()`: Main entry point (called during auth resolution)
   - `getTokenWithRefresh()`: Retrieves tokens and refreshes expired ones
   - `refreshAccessToken()`: OAuth token refresh logic
   - Reads from `~/.config/entire/hosts.json` for active user configuration
   - All these functions are testable without keyring because file-based storage is used

4. **auth_test.go** (695 lines)
   - Comprehensive test suite for auth functionality
   - Tests cover token encoding/decoding, credential parsing, endpoint handling
   - **Does NOT require system keyring** - uses `ENTIRE_TOKEN_STORE=file` in all tests
   - Tests `LookupEntireDBCredential()` with mock HTTP server for token refresh

### 3. Tests Related to Authentication/Credentials

**Direct authentication tests:**
- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/auth/auth_test.go` - All 25+ test functions
- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/auth_test.go` - 5 tests:
  - `TestResolveAuthMethodPrefersExplicitToken`
  - `TestNewConnSkipTLSVerify`
  - `TestNewConnUsesProvidedHTTPClient`
  - `TestResolveAuthMethodUsesEntireDBStoredToken`
  - `TestResolveAuthMethodRefreshesExpiredEntireDBToken`

**Files with auth-related helpers:**
- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/entire_local_smoke_test_test.go` - Tests Entire hosts file parsing
- `/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/internal/syncer/entire_local_smoke_test.go` - E2E smoke test (skipped by default)

### 4. Build Tags and Test Gates

**No Go build tags found.** Instead, tests use environment variable gating:

**Tests gated behind environment variables (skipped by default):**
1. `internal/syncer/entire_local_smoke_test.go::TestRun_EntireLocalPublicRepoSmoke` - Requires `GITSYNC_E2E_ENTIRE=1`
2. Multiple git-http-backend tests in `internal/syncer/git_http_backend_test.go` - Require `GITSYNC_E2E_GIT_HTTP_BACKEND=1`
3. `internal/syncer/live_bootstrap_test.go` - Requires `GITSYNC_E2E_LIVE_LINUX=1`

### 5. Tests Running Under Plain `go test ./...`

**Under default `go test ./...` execution:**
- All unit tests run normally (from `internal/auth/auth_test.go` and related unit test files)
- All integration tests in `internal/syncer/integration_test.go` run (they use in-memory git repos and httptest servers, no external dependencies)
- E2E/smoke tests are **SKIPPED** due to environment variable checks
- **NO SYSTEM KEYRING IS ACCESSED** because:
  - Unit tests use `ENTIRE_TOKEN_STORE=file` to force file-based storage
  - Integration tests use in-memory storage
  - E2E tests are gated and skipped by default

**Key behaviors:**
- Tests do NOT fail if keyring is unavailable
- Tests use temporary directories for file-based token storage
- Test credentials are mocked via HTTP test servers or environment setup
- The auth package gracefully handles both keyring and file backends

### 6. Keyring Integration Points

The go-keyring library is used in production code:
- **Default behavior**: `tokenstore.go` calls `keyring.Get()` and `keyring.Set()` when `ENTIRE_TOKEN_STORE != "file"`
- **Test behavior**: Forces file-based storage to avoid system keyring dependency
- **Dependency**: `github.com/zalando/go-keyring v0.2.8` in go.mod

**Summary**: The codebase is well-designed for testing. It abstracts keyring behind an interface, allows switching to file-based storage, and all default unit tests run without requiring system keyring.
```

```
5	
6	# Check for multi-line run blocks (run = """ with content spanning 3+ lines)
7	# These should be extracted to mise-tasks/ scripts
8	
9	awk '
10	/^[ \t]*run = """/ || /^[ \t]*run = '\'''\'''\''/ {
11	    in_block = 1
12	    start_line = NR
13	    lines = 0
14	    next
15	}
16	in_block {
17	    # Check for closing delimiter first (dont count it)
18	    if (/^[ \t]*"""/ || /^[ \t]*'\'''\'''\''/) {
19	        in_block = 0
20	        if (lines >= 3) {
21	            errors++
22	            printf "mise.toml:%d: multi-line script (%d lines) should be extracted to mise-tasks/\n", start_line, lines
23	        }
24	        next
25	    }
26	    lines++
27	}
28	END {
29	    if (errors > 0) {
30	        exit 1
31	    }
32	}
33	' mise.toml
```

> AGENT

That's ~330 real violations (the ones like `sb`, `buf` etc. are grep false positives). The breakdown of the significant ones:

| Linter | Count | Effort |
|--------|-------|--------|
| `wrapcheck` | 114 | Medium — needs `fmt.Errorf("...: %w", err)` wrapping |
| `perfsprint` | 50 | Easy — mechanical `fmt.Errorf` → `errors.New`, `fmt.Sprintf` → concat |
| `errcheck` | 45 | Easy/Medium — add `_ =` or handle errors |
| `intrange` | 25 | Easy — `for i := 0; i < n; i++` → `for i := range n` |
| `forbidigo` | 14 | Needs review — likely `os.Getwd` or similar banned calls |
| `revive` | 13 | Easy — style fixes |
| `inamedparam` | 11 | Easy — name interface params |
| `embeddedstructfieldcheck` | 9 | Easy — add blank lines in structs |
| Others | ~40 | Mixed |

Fixing all 330 in one go is doable but it's a big diff. A few options:

1. **Fix them all now** — one big cleanup PR, then the gates enforce a clean baseline
2. **Use golangci-lint's `--new-from-rev=HEAD`** — only lint new/changed code in PRs, then fix existing violations incrementally
3. **Fix the easy mechanical ones now**, disable the noisy ones (`wrapcheck`, `errcheck`) temporarily, enable them as you clean up

What do you prefer?

```
1	package syncer
2	
3	import (
4		"fmt"
5		"io"
6		"net/http"
7		"strings"
8		"sync"
9	
10		"github.com/entirehq/git-sync/internal/gitproto"
11	)
12	
13	// ServiceStats tracks transfer statistics for a single service.
14	type ServiceStats struct {
15		Name          string `json:"name"`
16		Requests      int    `json:"requests"`
17		RequestBytes  int64  `json:"request_bytes"`
18		ResponseBytes int64  `json:"response_bytes"`
19		Wants         int    `json:"wants"`
20		Haves         int    `json:"haves"`
21		Commands      int    `json:"commands"`
22	}
23	
24	// Stats holds the collected transfer statistics.
25	type Stats struct {
26		Enabled bool                     `json:"enabled"`
27		Items   map[string]*ServiceStats `json:"items"`
28	}
29	
30	// statsCollector is a concurrency-safe stats collector (issue #8).
31	type statsCollector struct {
32		enabled bool
33		mu      sync.Mutex
34		items   map[string]*ServiceStats
35	}
36	
37	func newStats(enabled bool) *statsCollector {
38		return &statsCollector{enabled: enabled, items: map[string]*ServiceStats{}}
39	}
40	
41	func (s *statsCollector) ensure(name string) *ServiceStats {
42		item, ok := s.items[name]
43		if !ok {
44			item = &ServiceStats{Name: name}
45			s.items[name] = item
46		}
47		return item
48	}
49	
50	func (s *statsCollector) recordRoundTrip(name string, requestBytes, responseBytes int64) {
51		if !s.enabled {
52			return
53		}
54		s.mu.Lock()
55		defer s.mu.Unlock()
56		item := s.ensure(name)
57		item.Requests++
58		item.RequestBytes += requestBytes
59		item.ResponseBytes += responseBytes
60	}
61	
62	func (s *statsCollector) snapshot() Stats {
63		s.mu.Lock()
64		defer s.mu.Unlock()
65		out := Stats{Enabled: s.enabled, Items: make(map[string]*ServiceStats, len(s.items))}
66		for key, item := range s.items {
67			copyItem := *item
68			out.Items[key] = &copyItem
69		}
70		return out
71	}
72	
73	// countingRoundTripper wraps an HTTP transport to record transfer stats.
74	type countingRoundTripper struct {
75		base  http.RoundTripper
76		label string
77		stats *statsCollector
78	}
79	
80	func (rt *countingRoundTripper) RoundTrip(req *http.Request) (*http.Response, error) {
81		res, err := rt.base.RoundTrip(req)
82		if err != nil {
83			return nil, fmt.Errorf("round trip: %w", err)
84		}
85	
86		serviceName := req.Header.Get(gitproto.StatsPhaseHeader)
87		if serviceName == "" {
88			serviceName = req.URL.Query().Get("service")
89			if serviceName == "" {
90				serviceName = strings.TrimPrefix(req.URL.Path[strings.LastIndex(req.URL.Path, "/")+1:], "/")
91			}
92		}
93		name := strings.TrimSpace(rt.label + " " + serviceName)
94		requestBytes := req.ContentLength
95		if requestBytes < 0 {
96			requestBytes = 0
97		}
98	
99		res.Body = &countingReadCloser{
100			ReadCloser: res.Body,
101			onClose: func(n int64) {
102				rt.stats.recordRoundTrip(name, requestBytes, n)
103			},
104		}
105		return res, nil
106	}
107	
108	type countingReadCloser struct {
109		io.ReadCloser
110	
111		n       int64
112		onClose func(int64)
113	}
114	
115	func (c *countingReadCloser) Read(p []byte) (int, error) {
116		n, err := c.ReadCloser.Read(p)
117		c.n += int64(n)
118		if err != nil {
119			return n, fmt.Errorf("read: %w", err)
120		}
121		return n, nil
122	}
123	
124	func (c *countingReadCloser) Close() error {
125		err := c.ReadCloser.Close()
126		if c.onClose != nil {
127			c.onClose(c.n)
128			c.onClose = nil
129		}
130		if err != nil {
131			return fmt.Errorf("close: %w", err)
132		}
133		return nil
134	}
135
```

```
1	package auth
2	
3	import (
4		"context"
5		"fmt"
6		"os/exec"
7		"strings"
8	
9		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/transport"
10		transporthttp "github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing/transport/http"
11	)
12	
13	const defaultGitUsername = "git"
14	
15	// Endpoint holds the authentication-related fields for a remote.
16	type Endpoint struct {
17		Username      string
18		Token         string
19		BearerToken   string
20		SkipTLSVerify bool
21	}
22	
23	// Resolve resolves the auth method for the given endpoint configuration.
24	// Order: explicit flags → Entire DB token → git credential helper → anonymous.
25	func Resolve(raw Endpoint, ep *transport.Endpoint) (transport.AuthMethod, error) {
26		if auth := explicitAuth(raw); auth != nil {
27			return auth, nil
28		}
29		if ep == nil {
30			return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil // nil signals no auth method found at this stage
31		}
32		if ep.Scheme != "http" && ep.Scheme != "https" {
33			return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil // nil signals no auth method found at this stage
34		}
35		if username, password, ok, err := LookupEntireDBCredential(raw, ep); err != nil {
36			return nil, err // issue #7: surface refresh failure explicitly
37		} else if ok {
38			return &transporthttp.BasicAuth{Username: username, Password: password}, nil
39		}
40		if username, password, ok := lookupGitCredential(ep); ok {
41			return &transporthttp.BasicAuth{Username: username, Password: password}, nil
42		}
43		return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil // nil signals no auth method found at this stage
44	}
45	
46	func explicitAuth(raw Endpoint) transport.AuthMethod {
47		if raw.BearerToken != "" {
48			return &transporthttp.TokenAuth{Token: raw.BearerToken}
49		}
50		if raw.Token != "" {
51			username := raw.Username
52			if username == "" {
53				username = defaultGitUsername
54			}
55			return &transporthttp.BasicAuth{Username: username, Password: raw.Token}
56		}
57		return nil
58	}
59	
60	// GitCredentialFillCommand is replaceable for testing.
61	var GitCredentialFillCommand = func(ctx context.Context, input string) ([]byte, error) {
62		cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "credential", "fill")
63		cmd.Stdin = strings.NewReader(input)
64		return cmd.Output()
65	}
66	
67	func lookupGitCredential(ep *transport.Endpoint) (string, string, bool) {
68		input := credentialFillInput(ep)
69		if input == "" {
70			return "", "", false
71		}
72		output, err := GitCredentialFillCommand(context.Background(), input)
73		if err != nil {
74			return "", "", false
75		}
76		values := parseCredentialOutput(output)
77		password := values["password"]
78		if password == "" {
79			return "", "", false
80		}
81		username := values["username"]
82		if username == "" {
83			if ep.User != nil && ep.User.Username() != "" {
84				username = ep.User.Username()
85			} else {
86				username = defaultGitUsername
87			}
88		}
89		return username, password, true
90	}
91	
92	func credentialFillInput(ep *transport.Endpoint) string {
93		if ep == nil || ep.Hostname() == "" {
94			return ""
95		}
96		var b strings.Builder
97		fmt.Fprintf(&b, "protocol=%s\nhost=%s\n", ep.Scheme, ep.Hostname())
98		if path := strings.TrimPrefix(ep.Path, "/"); path != "" {
99			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "path=%s\n", path)
100		}
101		if ep.User != nil && ep.User.Username() != "" {
102			fmt.Fprintf(&b, "username=%s\n", ep.User.Username())
103		}
104		b.WriteString("\n")
105		return b.String()
106	}
107	
108	func parseCredentialOutput(output []byte) map[string]string {
109		values := map[string]string{}
110		for _, line := range strings.Split(string(output), "\n") {
111			line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
112			if line == "" {
113				continue
114			}
115			k, v, ok := strings.Cut(line, "=")
116			if ok {
117				values[k] = v
118			}
119		}
120		return values
121	}
122
```

```
1	package gitproto
2	
3	import (
4		"errors"

8		"github.com/go-git/go-git/v6/plumbing"

10	
11	// ToPushCommands converts a slice of PushPlans to PushCommands.
12	// Used by all strategy packages to avoid copy-pasting the conversion.
13	func ToPushCommands(plans []PushPlan) []PushCommand {
14		cmds := make([]PushCommand, 0, len(plans))
15		for _, p := range plans {
16			cmd := PushCommand{Name: p.TargetRef, Old: p.TargetHash}
17			if p.Delete {
18				cmd.Delete = true
19			} else {
20				cmd.New = p.SourceHash
21			}
22			cmds = append(cmds, cmd)
23		}
24		return cmds
25	}
26	
27	// PushPlan is a minimal interface for plan-to-command conversion.
28	type PushPlan struct {
29		TargetRef  plumbing.ReferenceName
30		TargetHash plumbing.Hash
31		SourceHash plumbing.Hash
32		Delete     bool
33	}
34	
35	// LimitPackReader wraps a ReadCloser with a byte limit. Shared across strategies.
36	func LimitPackReader(r io.ReadCloser, maxBytes int64) io.ReadCloser {
37		if maxBytes <= 0 {
38			return r
39		}
40		return &packLimitRC{ReadCloser: r, max: maxBytes}
41	}
42	
43	type packLimitRC struct {
44		io.ReadCloser
45	
46		max  int64
47		read int64
48	}
49	
50	func (r *packLimitRC) Read(p []byte) (int, error) {
51		n, err := r.ReadCloser.Read(p)
52		r.read += int64(n)
53		if r.read > r.max {
54			return n, fmt.Errorf("source pack exceeded max-pack-bytes limit (%d)", r.max)
55		}
56		if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, io.EOF) {
57			return n, fmt.Errorf("read: %w", err)
58		}
59		return n, err
60	}
61
```

> DEVELOPER

1. High: internal/strategy/bootstrap/bootstrap.go:458 likely regresses peak-memory
     behavior by removing graphStore = nil before the explicit runtime.GC(). The
     surrounding comment says this store can reach roughly 4.6 GB for large repos and
     should be discarded immediately after extracting the first-parent chain. Without
     clearing the last local reference, the compiler/runtime may keep graphStore live until
     function exit, defeating the intended early reclamation.
  2. Medium: pkg/gitsync/types.go:68 changes the JSON wire format of exported stable API
     types. Adding tags to RefMapping, RefScope, and SyncPolicy changes serialized names
     from Source/Branches/IncludeTags/Protocol to source/branches/includeTags/protocol.
     That is a real compatibility change for any external caller persisting or exchanging
     these structs as JSON, and it also creates a mixed-casing payload because the outer
     request structs still use default field names.
  3. Low: pkg/gitsync/client.go:39 and pkg/gitsync/internalbridge/config.go:87 now double-
     wrap probe errors, producing messages like probe: probe: .... That does not break
     behavior, but it degrades error quality and makes the layering look accidental. The
     bridge is the better place to remove wrapping, since it is mostly pass-through.
  4. Low: pkg/gitsync/unstable/client.go:37 also changes JSON field names for exported
     unstable.AdvancedOptions (CollectStats -> collectStats, etc.). This is less serious
     because the package is explicitly unstable, but it is still a public serialization
     change, not just a lint cleanup.

```
453		graphStore := memory.NewStorage()
```

```
30	// Probe inspects a source remote and optional target remote.
31	func (c *Client) Probe(ctx context.Context, req ProbeRequest) (ProbeResult, error) {
32		if err := req.Validate(); err != nil {
33			return ProbeResult{}, err
34		}
35		cfg, err := c.buildProbeConfig(ctx, req)
36		if err != nil {
37			return ProbeResult{}, err
38		}
39		result, err := internalbridge.Probe(ctx, cfg)
40		if err != nil {
41			return ProbeResult{}, fmt.Errorf("probe: %w", err)
42		}
43		return internalbridge.FromProbeResult(result), nil
44	}
45	
46	// Plan computes ref actions without pushing.
47	func (c *Client) Plan(ctx context.Context, req PlanRequest) (PlanResult, error) {
48		if err := req.Validate(); err != nil {
49			return PlanResult{}, err
50		}
51		cfg, err := c.buildSyncConfig(ctx, req.Source, req.Target, req.Scope, req.Policy, req.CollectStats, true)
52		if err != nil {
53			return PlanResult{}, err
54		}
55		result, err := internalbridge.Run(ctx, cfg)
56		if err != nil {
57			return PlanResult{}, fmt.Errorf("plan: %w", err)
58		}
59		return internalbridge.FromSyncResult(result), nil
60	}
61	
62	// Sync executes a sync between two remotes.
63	func (c *Client) Sync(ctx context.Context, req SyncRequest) (SyncResult, error) {
64		if err := req.Validate(); err != nil {
65			return SyncResult{}, err
66		}
67		cfg, err := c.buildSyncConfig(ctx, req.Source, req.Target, req.Scope, req.Policy, req.CollectStats, false)
68		if err != nil {
69			return SyncResult{}, err
70		}
71		result, err := internalbridge.Run(ctx, cfg)
72		if err != nil {
73			return SyncResult{}, fmt.Errorf("sync: %w", err)
74		}
75		return internalbridge.FromSyncResult(result), nil
76	}
```

Everything is clean. All four issues are fixed:

1. **Restored `graphStore = nil`** with `//nolint:ineffassign,wastedassign` — preserves the ~4.6 GB early reclamation before `runtime.GC()`
2. **Reverted JSON tags on stable API types** (`RefMapping`, `RefScope`, `SyncPolicy`) — no wire-format change
3. **Removed double-wrapping** in `internalbridge` — the bridge is now pass-through, `pkg/gitsync/client.go` adds the only context layer
4. **Reverted JSON tags on `unstable.AdvancedOptions`** — no serialization change; `musttag` suppressed on the bench tool's debug output instead

Ready for commit and PR whenever you want.

```
## JSON Serialization Audit Report

### Summary
The codebase has **significant inconsistency** in JSON serialization. Some structs are explicitly tagged with `snake_case` JSON tags, others rely on default Go JSON encoding (PascalCase), and most input/configuration structs have NO tags at all. The serialized output types show a consistent `snake_case` convention while configuration input types are untagged.

---

### Package 1: `pkg/gitsync/` - Stable Public API

#### File: `pkg/gitsync/types.go`

| Struct | JSON Tags | Convention | Notes |
|--------|-----------|-----------|-------|
| `Endpoint` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal as `{"URL": "..."}` |
| `EndpointAuth` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal as `{"Username": "...", "Token": "...", ...}` |
| `StaticAuthProvider` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal as `{"Source": {...}, "Target": {...}}` |
| `RefMapping` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal as `{"Source": "...", "Target": "..."}` |
| `RefScope` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal as `{"Branches": [...], "Mappings": [...]}` |
| `SyncPolicy` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal as `{"Mode": "...", "IncludeTags": true, ...}` |
| `ProbeRequest` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal with PascalCase |
| `PlanRequest` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal with PascalCase |
| `SyncRequest` | NONE | Default PascalCase | Would marshal with PascalCase |

#### File: `pkg/gitsync/client.go`

| Struct | JSON Tags | Convention | Notes |
|--------|-----------|-----------|-------|

## Serialization Flow Analysis

**Which structs actually get serialized via json.Marshal:**

1. **cmd/git-sync/main.go** line 372: `json.MarshalIndent(value, "", "  ")`
   - Called via `printOutput(jsonOutput bool, value interface{ Lines() []string })`
   - Receives: `Result`, `ProbeResult`, `FetchResult` from `unstable` package
   - These are type aliases to `syncer` types

2. **All serialized types consistently use snake_case** through explicit tags or custom MarshalJSON

---

## Inconsistency Summary

### CRITICAL INCONSISTENCIES:

1. **Input/Configuration Structs (Untagged):**
   - `Endpoint`, `EndpointAuth`, `RefMapping`, `RefScope`, `SyncPolicy` in `pkg/gitsync/` 
   - `Endpoint`, `EndpointAuth`, `RefMapping`, `RefScope`, `SyncPolicy` in `internalbridge/config.go`
   - All Request structs: `ProbeRequest`, `PlanRequest`, `SyncRequest`
   - All Options structs: `Options`, `AdvancedOptions`
   - Would marshal with **PascalCase** field names if ever serialized

2. **Output/Result Structs (Tagged snake_case):**
   - `Result`, `ProbeResult`, `FetchResult`, `RefInfo` in `syncer`
   - `RefResult`, `SyncCounts`, `BatchSummary`, `ExecutionSummary`, `SyncResult` in `internalbridge`
   - Consistently use **snake_case** tags

3. **Bridge Layer Duplication:**
   - `internalbridge/model.go` duplicates `syncer` result types with identical structure and snake_case tags
   - Creates maintenance burden (same tags in two places)

### CASING CONVENTION:
- **snake_case**: Used for all serialized output types (Results, Stats, Measurement, Plan details)
- **PascalCase (default)**: Applied implicitly to all untagged structs (configuration input types)
- **No mixed case** within any tagged struct (consistent within each struct)

---

## Recommendation Areas

1. **Configuration input types** (Request, Options, Endpoint, Auth, etc.) are unlikely to be serialized but currently rely on Go's default PascalCase encoding if they ever are
2. **Duplication** between `syncer` and `internalbridge` result types increases maintenance complexity
3. **Custom MarshalJSON** in `RefInfo`, `FetchResult`, and `BranchPlan` handles Go type conversions (Hash to string, ReferenceName to string)
```

> AGENT

Here's the picture:

**Output types** (Result, ProbeResult, RefResult, Stats, etc.) — all consistently `snake_case` tagged. These are what actually hits `json.Marshal` in `cmd/git-sync/main.go`.

**Input/config types** (Endpoint, RefMapping, RefScope, SyncPolicy, AdvancedOptions, all Request structs) — **no tags at all**. These default to PascalCase if ever marshaled. Currently they're only used programmatically (never serialized), but if a caller ever did marshal a `SyncRequest`, they'd get a mix of PascalCase config fields containing snake_case result fields.

Two options:

1. **Tag the input types with `snake_case`** to match the output types — consistent wire format everywhere
2. **Leave input types untagged** since they're never serialized today — accept the asymmetry

Given this is a new project and you're fine changing the stable API, option 1 seems right — adds consistency now before more consumers arrive. Want me to go ahead and add `snake_case` json tags to all the untagged public structs?

> DEVELOPER

is snake_case usually what go projects use for json? or is there no good best practice?

> AGENT

The Go standard library defaults to PascalCase (the field name as-is), but the overwhelming convention in the Go ecosystem is **`camelCase`** for JSON — matching what JavaScript/JSON consumers expect. The Go blog, Google APIs, Kubernetes, Docker, GitHub's Go SDKs, and most popular Go projects all use `camelCase` json tags.

`snake_case` is less common in Go but shows up in projects that interop with Python/Ruby ecosystems or follow specific API style guides (like some older Google APIs).

So your codebase has a split: the output types already use `snake_case`, and adding tags to input types would mean committing to that convention project-wide. 

The question is whether you want to:
1. **Standardize on `snake_case`** — stay consistent with what the output types already do
2. **Standardize on `camelCase`** — follow Go ecosystem convention, but requires migrating the existing output types too (breaking change for any current consumers of the JSON output from `git-sync` CLI)

Since this is early and you said there's only one external dependency — now is the cheapest time to switch to `camelCase` everywhere if that's what you'd prefer. What's your call?

> DEVELOPER

let's switch to camelCase

```
273	func captureStdout(fn func() error) (string, error) {
274		old := os.Stdout
275		r, w, err := os.Pipe()
276		if err != nil {
277			return "", err
278		}
279		os.Stdout = w
280		defer func() { os.Stdout = old }()
281	
282		runErr := fn()
283		_ = w.Close()
284	
285		var buf bytes.Buffer
286		if _, err := io.Copy(&buf, r); err != nil {
287			return "", err
288		}
289		_ = r.Close()
290		return strings.TrimSpace(buf.String()), runErr
291	}
292	
293	func newSourceRepo(t *testing.T) (*git.Repository, billy.Filesystem) {
294		t.Helper()
295	
296		fs := memfs.New()
297		repo, err := git.Init(memory.NewStorage(), git.WithWorkTree(fs))
298		if err != nil {
299			t.Fatalf("init source repo: %v", err)
300		}
301	
302		return repo, fs
303	}
304	
305	func makeCommits(t *testing.T, repo *git.Repository, fs billy.Filesystem, count int) {
306		t.Helper()
307	
308		wt, err := repo.Worktree()
309		if err != nil {
310			t.Fatalf("open worktree: %v", err)
311		}
312	
313		for i := range count {
314			content := strings.Repeat(fmt.Sprintf("line %d %d\n", i, time.Now().UnixNano()), 24)
315			file, err := fs.Create("tracked.txt")
316			if err != nil {
317				t.Fatalf("create file: %v", err)
318			}
319			if _, err := io.WriteString(file, content); err != nil {
320				t.Fatalf("write file: %v", err)
321			}
322			if err := file.Close(); err != nil {
323				t.Fatalf("close file: %v", err)
324			}
325	
326			if _, err := wt.Add("tracked.txt"); err != nil {
327				t.Fatalf("add file: %v", err)
328			}
329	
330			_, err = wt.Commit(fmt.Sprintf("commit %d", i), &git.CommitOptions{
331				Author:    &objectSignature,
332				Committer: &objectSignature,
333			})
334			if err != nil {
335				t.Fatalf("commit: %v", err)
336			}
337		}
338	}
339	
340	var objectSignature = signature()
341	
342	func signature() object.Signature {
343		return object.Signature{
344			Name:  "test",
345			Email: "test@example.com",
346			When:  time.Unix(1, 0).UTC(),
347		}
348	}
349	
350	type smartHTTPRepoServer struct {
351		t        *testing.T
352		server   *httptest.Server
353		repo     *git.Repository
354		repoPath string
355	
356		mu           sync.Mutex
357		receivePacks int
358		thinCapable  bool
359	}
360	
361	func newSmartHTTPRepoServer(t *testing.T, repo *git.Repository) *smartHTTPRepoServer {
362		t.Helper()
363	
364		s := &smartHTTPRepoServer{
365			t:           t,
366			repo:        repo,
367			repoPath:    "/repo.git",
368			thinCapable: true,
369		}
370		s.server = httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(s.handle))
371		return s
372	}
373	
374	func (s *smartHTTPRepoServer) Close() {
375		s.server.Close()
376	}
377	
378	func (s *smartHTTPRepoServer) RepoURL() string {
379		return s.server.URL + s.repoPath
380	}
381	
382	func (s *smartHTTPRepoServer) Count(service string) int {
383		s.mu.Lock()
384		defer s.mu.Unlock()
385		if service == "git-receive-pack" {
386			return s.receivePacks
387		}
388		return 0
389	}
390	
391	func (s *smartHTTPRepoServer) handle(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
392		switch {
393		case r.Method == http.MethodGet && r.URL.Path == s.repoPath+"/info/refs":
394			s.handleInfoRefs(w, r)
395		case r.Method == http.MethodPost && r.URL.Path == s.repoPath+"/git-upload-pack":
396			s.handleUploadPack(w, r)
397		case r.Method == http.MethodPost && r.URL.Path == s.repoPath+"/git-receive-pack":
398			s.handleReceivePack(w, r)
399		default:
400			http.NotFound(w, r)
401		}
402	}
403	
404	func (s *smartHTTPRepoServer) handleInfoRefs(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
405		service := transport.Service(r.URL.Query().Get("service"))
406		if service != transport.UploadPackService && service != transport.ReceivePackService {
407			http.Error(w, "missing service", http.StatusBadRequest)
408			return
409		}
410	
411		var buf bytes.Buffer
412		if err := transport.AdvertiseReferences(r.Context(), s.repo.Storer, &buf, service, false); err != nil {
413			http.Error(w, err.Error(), http.StatusInternalServerError)
414			return
415		}
416		if service == transport.ReceivePackService && s.thinCapable {
417			rewritten, err := rewriteReceivePackAdvertisement(buf.Bytes(), func(caps *capability.List) {
418				caps.Delete(capability.Capability("no-thin"))
419			})
420			if err != nil {
421				s.t.Fatalf("rewrite receive-pack advertisement: %v", err)
422			}
423			buf.Reset()
424			_, _ = buf.Write(rewritten)
425		}
426	
427		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", fmt.Sprintf("application/x-%s-advertisement", service))
428		if _, err := w.Write(buf.Bytes()); err != nil {
429			s.t.Fatalf("write advertised refs: %v", err)
430		}
431	}
432	
433	func rewriteReceivePackAdvertisement(data []byte, mutate func(*capability.List)) ([]byte, error) {
434		ar := packp.NewAdvRefs()
435		if err := ar.Decode(bytes.NewReader(data)); err == nil {
436			mutate(ar.Capabilities)
437			var buf bytes.Buffer
438			if err := ar.Encode(&buf); err != nil {
439				return nil, err
440			}
441			return buf.Bytes(), nil
442		}
443	
444		rd := bytes.NewReader(data)
445		var smart packp.SmartReply
446		if err := smart.Decode(rd); err != nil {
447			return nil, err
448		}
449		ar = packp.NewAdvRefs()
450		if err := ar.Decode(rd); err != nil {
451			return nil, err
452		}
453		mutate(ar.Capabilities)
454		var buf bytes.Buffer
455		if err := smart.Encode(&buf); err != nil {
456			return nil, err
457		}
458		if err := ar.Encode(&buf); err != nil {
459			return nil, err
460		}
461		return buf.Bytes(), nil
462	}
463	
464	func (s *smartHTTPRepoServer) handleUploadPack(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
465		var buf bytes.Buffer
466		wc := nopWriteCloser{&buf}
467	
468		err := transport.UploadPack(r.Context(), s.repo.Storer, r.Body, wc, &transport.UploadPackOptions{
469			StatelessRPC: true,
470		})
471		if err != nil {
472			http.Error(w, err.Error(), http.StatusInternalServerError)
473			return
474		}
475	
476		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/x-git-upload-pack-result")
477		if _, err := w.Write(buf.Bytes()); err != nil {
478			s.t.Fatalf("write upload-pack response: %v", err)
479		}
480	}
481	
482	func (s *smartHTTPRepoServer) handleReceivePack(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
483		s.mu.Lock()
484		s.receivePacks++
485		s.mu.Unlock()
486	
487		var buf bytes.Buffer
488		wc := nopWriteCloser{&buf}
489	
490		err := transport.ReceivePack(r.Context(), s.repo.Storer, r.Body, wc, &transport.ReceivePackOptions{
491			StatelessRPC: true,
492		})
493	
494		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/x-git-receive-pack-result")
495		if buf.Len() > 0 {
496			if _, err := w.Write(buf.Bytes()); err != nil {
497				s.t.Fatalf("write receive-pack response: %v", err)
498			}
499		}
```

```
Based on my thorough search of README.md and all documentation files in the docs/ directory, here is the complete list of snake_case JSON field references that need updating to camelCase:

## Summary of snake_case JSON References Requiring camelCase Updates

### README.md

**Line 350-356:**
- Line 350: `keys use \`snake_case\`` - This is a comment explaining current behavior
- Line 352: `source_url` → should be `sourceUrl`
- Line 352: `target_url` → should be `targetUrl`
- Line 352: `ref_prefixes` → should be `refPrefixes`
- Line 352: `source_capabilities` → should be `sourceCapabilities`
- Line 352: `target_capabilities` → should be `targetCapabilities`
- Line 353: `source_url` → should be `sourceUrl`
- Line 353: `fetched_objects` → should be `fetchedObjects`
- Line 354: `dry_run` → should be `dryRun`
- Line 355: `relay_mode` → should be `relayMode`
- Line 355: `relay_reason` → should be `relayReason`
- Line 355: `batch_count` → should be `batchCount`
- Line 355: `planned_batch_count` → should be `plannedBatchCount`
- Line 355: `temp_refs` → should be `tempRefs`
- Line 356: `source_ref` → should be `sourceRef`
- Line 356: `target_ref` → should be `targetRef`
- Line 356: `source_hash` → should be `sourceHash`

### docs/embedding.md

**Line 122-123:**
- Line 122: `operation_mode` → should be `operationMode`
- Line 123: `transfer_mode` → should be `transferMode`

## Key Findings

All snake_case JSON field references are documented in:
1. **README.md** (lines 350-356) - The JSON Output section documenting the API contract
2. **docs/embedding.md** (lines 122-123) - The Result Handling section for worker consumption

Additionally, the comment at line 350 of README.md itself states `keys use \`snake_case\`` which explains the current behavior and should be updated to reflect the migration to camelCase.

These are the only files with documented JSON field references. The other docs (architecture.md, benchmarking.md, bootstrap.md, bootstrap-batching.md, testing.md) do not contain JSON field examples that need updating—they discuss architectural concepts, benchmarking, and testing rather than JSON output specifications.
```

340	```bash
341	go run ./cmd/git-sync plan --stats <source-url> <target-url>
342	```
343	
344	## JSON Output
345	
346	Add `--json` to `probe`, `fetch`, `bootstrap`, `plan`, or `sync` to emit machine-readable output instead of the default text format.
347	
348	The JSON interface is intentionally stable:
349	
350	- keys use `snake_case`
351	- refs and hashes are serialized as strings, not raw byte arrays
352	- `probe` returns top-level keys such as `source_url`, `target_url`, `protocol`, `ref_prefixes`, `source_capabilities`, `target_capabilities`, `refs`, and `stats`
353	- `fetch` returns top-level keys such as `source_url`, `protocol`, `wants`, `haves`, `fetched_objects`, and `stats`
354	- `bootstrap`, `plan`, and `sync` return top-level keys such as `plans`, `pushed`, `skipped`, `blocked`, `deleted`, `dry_run`, `protocol`, and `stats`
355	- `bootstrap`, `plan`, and `sync` also expose `relay`, `relay_mode`, `relay_reason`, `batching`, `batch_count`, `planned_batch_count`, and `temp_refs`
356	- each item in `plans` includes stable string fields such as `branch`, `source_ref`, `target_ref`, `source_hash`, `target_hash`, `kind`, `action`, and `reason`
357	
358	## Auth
359	
360	For GitHub and similar providers, use basic auth with a token as the password.
361	
362	Auth is resolved in this order:
363	
364	- explicit CLI flags
365	- `GITSYNC_*` environment variables
366	- local `git credential fill` helper lookup for `http` and `https` remotes
367	- anonymous access
368	
369	Relevant variables:

```
115	The stable `SyncResult` is organized for worker consumption:
116	
117	- `Refs`
118	  per-ref outcomes and reasons
119	- `Counts`
120	  aggregate applied/skipped/blocked/deleted totals
121	- `Execution`
122	  protocol, `operation_mode` (sync or replicate), relay summary,
123	  `transfer_mode` (the engine path that executed), and batch summary
124	- `Stats`
125	  transfer counters when requested
126	- `Measurement`
127	  only where exposed by the stable surface
128	
129	That gives a worker enough structure to:
130	
131	- persist job history
132	- emit metrics
133	- log ref-level outcomes
134	- make retry/escalation decisions
```

2. Low: .github/workflows/ci.yml:12 and .github/workflows/lint.yml:16 set actions/
     checkout to ref: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha }} even though both workflows
     also run on push and workflow_dispatch. Outside PR events that field is unset, so the
     checkout behavior depends on how actions/checkout treats an empty ref. It is safer to
     omit ref entirely or gate it to PR events only.

Good catch. Simplest fix — just drop the `ref` entirely. The default checkout behavior already does the right thing for all three triggers (checks out the merge commit on PRs, the pushed commit on push, the default branch on dispatch).

```
1	#!/bin/bash
2	#MISE description="Check dependency licenses"
3	set -e
4	
5	# Colors for output
6	RED='\033[0;31m'
7	GREEN='\033[0;32m'
8	YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
9	NC='\033[0m' # No Color
10	
11	echo "Checking dependency licenses..."
12	
13	# Install go-licenses if not available
14	if ! command -v go-licenses &> /dev/null; then
15	    echo "Installing go-licenses tool..."
16	    go install github.com/google/go-licenses@latest
17	    GOPATH=$(go env GOPATH)
18	    export PATH="$GOPATH/bin:$PATH"
19	fi
20	
21	# Load allowed licenses
22	ALLOWED_LICENSES_FILE=".allowed-licenses"
23	if [ ! -f "$ALLOWED_LICENSES_FILE" ]; then
24	    echo -e "${RED}ERROR: .allowed-licenses file not found${NC}"
25	    exit 1
26	fi
27	
28	# Read allowed licenses into array (skip comments and empty lines)
29	ALLOWED_LICENSES=()
30	while IFS= read -r line; do
31	    # Skip comments and empty lines
32	    if [[ ! "$line" =~ ^[[:space:]]*# ]] && [[ -n "$line" ]]; then
33	        ALLOWED_LICENSES+=("$line")
34	    fi
35	done < "$ALLOWED_LICENSES_FILE"
36	
37	echo "Allowed licenses: ${ALLOWED_LICENSES[*]}"
38	echo ""
39	
40	# Get licenses from dependencies
41	TEMP_FILE=$(mktemp)
42	# shellcheck disable=SC2312
43	go-licenses csv ./cmd/entire 2>/dev/null | sort | uniq > "$TEMP_FILE"
44	
45	# Check each dependency
46	VIOLATIONS=()
47	while IFS=',' read -r package _url license; do
48	    # Skip internal packages
49	    if [[ "$package" == entire.io/* ]]; then
50	        continue
51	    fi
52	
53	    # Check if license is allowed
54	    ALLOWED=false
55	    for allowed in "${ALLOWED_LICENSES[@]}"; do
56	        if [ "$license" == "$allowed" ]; then
57	            ALLOWED=true
58	            break
59	        fi
60	    done
61	
62	    if [ "$ALLOWED" = false ]; then
63	        VIOLATIONS+=("$package,$license")
64	    fi
65	done < "$TEMP_FILE"
66	
67	# Report results
68	if [ ${#VIOLATIONS[@]} -eq 0 ]; then
69	    echo -e "${GREEN}✓ All dependency licenses are allowed${NC}"
70	    rm "$TEMP_FILE"
71	    exit 0
72	else
73	    echo -e "${RED}✗ Found ${#VIOLATIONS[@]} dependency(ies) with non-allowed licenses:${NC}"
74	    echo ""
75	    for violation in "${VIOLATIONS[@]}"; do
76	        IFS=',' read -r pkg lic <<< "$violation"
77	        echo -e "${YELLOW}  - $pkg${NC}"
78	        echo -e "    License: ${RED}$lic${NC}"
79	    done
80	    echo ""
81	    echo "If these licenses are acceptable, add them to .allowed-licenses"
82	    rm "$TEMP_FILE"
83	    exit 1
84	fi
85	
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/git-sync/mise-tasks/lint/licenses",
  "content": "#!/bin/bash\n#MISE description=\"Check dependency licenses\"\nset -e\n\n# Colors for output\nRED='\\033[0;31m'\nGREEN='\\033[0;32m'\nYELLOW='\\033[1;33m'\nNC='\\033[0m' # No Color\n\necho \"Checking dependency licenses...\"\n\n# Install go-licenses if not available\nif ! command -v go-licenses &> /dev/null; then\n    echo \"Installing go-licenses tool...\"\n    go install github.com/google/go-licenses@latest\n    GOPATH=$(go env GOPATH)\n    export PATH=\"$GOPATH/bin:$PATH\"\nfi\n\n# Load allowed licenses\nALLOWED_LICENSES_FILE=\".allowed-licenses\"\nif [ ! -f \"$ALLOWED_LICENSES_FILE\" ]; then\n    echo -e \"${RED}ERROR: .allowed-licenses file not found${NC}\"\n    exit 1\nfi\n\n# Read allowed licenses into array (skip comments and empty lines)\nALLOWED_LICENSES=()\nwhile IFS= read -r line; do\n    # Skip comments and empty lines\n    if [[ ! \"$line\" =~ ^[[:space:]]*# ]] && [[ -n \"$line\" ]]; then\n        ALLOWED_LICENSES+=(\"$line\")\n    fi\ndone < \"$ALLOWED_LICENSES_FILE\"\n\necho \"Allowed licenses: ${ALLOWED_LICENSES[*]}\"\necho \"\"\n\n# Get licenses from dependencies\nTEMP_FILE=$(mktemp)\n# shellcheck disable=SC2312\ngo-licenses csv ./cmd/git-sync 2>/dev/null | sort | uniq > \"$TEMP_FILE\"\n\n# Check each dependency\nVIOLATIONS=()\nwhile IFS=',' read -r package _url license; do\n    # Skip internal packages\n    if [[ \"$package\" == github.com/entirehq/git-sync/* ]]; then\n        continue\n    fi\n\n    # Check if license is allowed\n    ALLOWED=false\n    for allowed in \"${ALLOWED_LICENSES[@]}\"; do\n        if [ \"$license\" == \"$allowed\" ]; then\n            ALLOWED=true\n            break\n        fi\n    done\n\n    if [ \"$ALLOWED\" = false ]; then\n        VIOLATIONS+=(\"$package,$license\")\n    fi\ndone < \"$TEMP_FILE\"\n\n# Report results\nif [ ${#VIOLATIONS[@]} -eq 0 ]; then\n    echo -e \"${GREEN}✓ All dependency licenses are allowed${NC}\"\n    rm \"$TEMP_FILE\"\n    exit 0\nelse\n    echo -e \"${RED}✗ Found ${#VIOLATIONS[@]} dependency(ies) with non-allowed licenses:${NC}\"\n    echo \"\"\n    for violation in \"${VIOLATIONS[@]}\"; do\n        IFS=',' read -r pkg lic <<< \"$violation\"\n        echo -e \"${YELLOW}  - $pkg${NC}\"\n        echo -e \"    License: ${RED}$lic${NC}\"\n    done\n    echo \"\"\n    echo \"If these licenses are acceptable, add them to .allowed-licenses\"\n    rm \"$TEMP_FILE\"\n    exit 1\nfi\n"
}
```

```
153			}
154		}))
155		defer server.Close()
156	
157		ep, err := transport.NewEndpoint(server.URL + "/git/test/repo")
158		if err != nil {
159			t.Fatalf("new endpoint: %v", err)
160		}
161		credHost := ep.Host
162		writeEntireDBHostsFile(t, configDir, credHost, "test-user")
163		// Expired token: expiration in the past
164		if err := auth.WriteStoredToken("entire:"+credHost, "test-user", "expired-token|1"); err != nil {
165			t.Fatalf("write expired token: %v", err)
166		}
167		if err := auth.WriteStoredToken("entire:"+credHost+":refresh", "test-user", "refresh-token"); err != nil {
168			t.Fatalf("write refresh token: %v", err)
169		}
```

> AGENT

That `Username for 'https://example.com':` is the system git credential helper prompting interactively. Tests should never block on that. The fix is to disable interactive git prompts during test runs.

> AGENT

`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` tells git to fail immediately instead of prompting for credentials. This prevents tests from hanging when a code path accidentally reaches the real credential helper.
